    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nStructured IE exhibits oracle scaling rates comparable to reasoning ($c{\\approx}0.5$; \\S\\ref{sec:scaling}), but confidence-based aggregation cannot close the resulting gap.\nThree factors constrain aggregation: degeneracy (17.8--67.9\\%), limited headroom, and LP-quality alignment ($\\rho$ 0.043--0.667).\nTemperature disentangles these: at $T{=}1.2$, degeneracy drops to 14.9\\% yet LP does not improve ($\\rho{=}0.225$; Table~\\ref{tab:t_degeneracy}); when LP compression is explicitly mitigated, LP yields $+2.9$~pp ($p{=}0.004$; Appendix~\\ref{app:epsilon_robustness}).\nThe selection window narrows with model improvement \\cite{wang2023selfconsistency, snell2025scaling}.\n\n\\paragraph{From diagnosis to practice.}\\label{sec:selection_method}\nEntity-level construction bypasses both failure mechanisms, with gains positive when degeneracy ${<}22$\\% and absent when ${>}31$\\% across seven conditions (all $p{<}0.01$; Appendix~\\ref{app:epoch_confound}). Under 72B few-shot, construction fails yet QE remains effective (SJ $\\rho$ 0.29--0.44; Appendix~\\ref{app:fs_error}), confirming quality estimation is regime-independent.\nAlternative strategies (DALS, MBR, DGS) and supervised signal mixtures fail to beat greedy (Appendix~\\ref{app:dals}, \\ref{app:supervised_combination}); these negative results are specific to fine-tuned models with constrained decoding (Appendix~\\ref{app:when_selection_works}).\nNER calibration is reasonable (ECE 0.069--0.096; Appendix~\\ref{app:calibration}).\n",

_source line 7409 · 2026-05-20T11:22:53.320Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01DmZRnE63VFiMN65QVbQQVd`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	Structured IE exhibits oracle scaling rates comparable to reasoning ($c{\approx}0.5$; \S\ref{sec:scaling}), but confidence-based aggregation cannot close the resulting gap.
4	Three factors constrain aggregation: degeneracy (17.8--67.9\%), limited headroom, and LP-quality alignment ($\rho$ 0.043--0.667).
5	Temperature disentangles these: at $T{=}1.2$, degeneracy drops to 14.9\% yet LP does not improve ($\rho{=}0.225$; Table~\ref{tab:t_degeneracy}); when LP compression is explicitly mitigated, LP yields $+2.9$~pp ($p{=}0.004$; Appendix~\ref{app:epsilon_robustness}).
6	The selection window narrows with model improvement \cite{wang2023selfconsistency, snell2025scaling}.
7	
8	\paragraph{From diagnosis to practice.}\label{sec:selection_method}
9	Entity-level construction bypasses both failure mechanisms, with gains positive when degeneracy ${<}22$\% and absent when ${>}31$\% across seven conditions (all $p{<}0.01$; Appendix~\ref{app:epoch_confound}). Under 72B few-shot, construction fails yet QE remains effective (SJ $\rho$ 0.29--0.44; Appendix~\ref{app:fs_error}), confirming quality estimation is regime-independent.
10	Alternative strategies (DALS, MBR, DGS) and supervised signal mixtures all fail to beat greedy (Appendix~\ref{app:dals}, \ref{app:supervised_combination}, \ref{app:when_selection_works}).
11	
```

</details>

Under 72B few-shot, construction fails yet QE remains effective (SJ $\\rho$ 0.29--0.44; Appendix~\\ref{app:fs_error}), confirming quality estimation is regime-independent."
        ]
      }
    ],
    "userModified": false,

    "oldString": "\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:mbr_construction}\n\\hat{y}_{\\textup{MBR}} {=} \\bigl\\{e {\\in} E_\\cup : \\hat{p}(e) {>} \\tfrac{\\alpha}{\\alpha{+}1}\\bigr\\},\\;\\; \\hat{p}(e) {=} \\tfrac{1}{N} \\textstyle\\sum\\nolimits_{i=1}^{N} \\mathbf{1}[e {\\in} y_i]\n\\end{equation}",
    "newString": "\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:mbr_construction}\n\\hat{y}_{\\textup{MBR}} \\!=\\! \\bigl\\{e \\!{\\in}\\! E_\\cup \\!:\\! \\hat{p}(e) \\!>\\! \\tfrac{\\alpha}{\\alpha{+}1}\\bigr\\},\\; \\hat{p}(e) \\!=\\! \\tfrac{1}{N} \\textstyle\\sum\\nolimits_{i} \\mathbf{1}[e \\!{\\in}\\! y_i]\n\\end{equation}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nGiven model $\\mathcal{M}$ and input $x$, we sample $N$ outputs $\\{y_1, \\ldots, y_N\\}$ at temperature $T > 0$ via constrained decoding \\cite{willard2023guided}, where each $y_i$ is an entity set.\nA \\textbf{per-sample signal} $c_{\\text{ps}}: (x, y_i) \\to \\mathbb{R}$ scores each output independently; an \\textbf{instance-level signal} $c_{\\text{il}}: (x, \\{y_i\\}_{i=1}^N) \\to \\mathbb{R}$ produces a single score per input (notation in Appendix~\\ref{app:notation}).\nThis distinction is consequential for selection: in our study, 4 of 5 signals are instance-level aggregates that assign identical QE scores to all $N$ candidates. Consensus-proximity (MBR) converts these to per-sample rankings (\\S\\ref{sec:selection_gap}), but these rankings do not improve selection.\nOnly LP carries per-sample discriminative power: ensemble optimization consistently assigns dominant weight to LP (mean $0.78$; Appendix~\\ref{app:supervised_combination}), with structural signals receiving weight only as secondary regularizers.\nWhen LP fails---as in few-shot regimes where LP $\\rho{\\to}0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:scaling})---no alternative per-sample signal exists, explaining why few-shot construction uniformly fails.\n\n\\subsection{Confidence Signals}\\label{sec:signals}\n\nThroughout, entity matching uses exact span and type match (a predicted entity is correct iff its character offsets and type label both match a gold entity).\nFour are \\textit{consistency signals}: \\textit{structural} (Soft Jaccard [SJ], entity voting confidence [VC]) and \\textit{surface} (exact match [EM], Fleiss' $\\kappa$ [FK]); log-probability [LP] is the sole \\textit{model-internal}, per-sample signal.\n\n\\paragraph{Log-probability (model-internal, per-sample).}\nFor sample $y_i$ with tokens $t_1, \\ldots, t_{|y_i|}$:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:logprob}\nc_{\\text{lp}}(y_i) = \\frac{1}{|y_i|} \\sum_{j=1}^{|y_i|} \\log p_{\\mathcal{M}}(t_j \\mid t_{&lt;j}, x)\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\paragraph{Exact match voting (surface, instance-level).}\nThe proportion of samples producing the most common output:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:em}\nc_{\\text{em}}(x) = \\frac{1}{N} \\max_{y} \\big|\\{i : y_i = y\\}\\big|\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\paragraph{Fleiss' $\\kappa$ (surface, instance-level).}\nWe adapt \\citet{fleiss1971agreement} to entity sets: items are unique (text, type) tuples across all $N$ samples; each sample assigns binary present/absent labels, yielding a $|E_\\cup| \\times 2$ rating matrix:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:fk}\n\\kappa = \\frac{\\bar{P} - \\bar{P}_e}{1 - \\bar{P}_e}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\bar{P}$ is mean observed and $\\bar{P}_e$ expected agreement.\nFK relies on exact string matching (independence analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:fk_independence}).\n\n\\paragraph{Soft Jaccard (structural, instance-level).}\nFor samples $y_i, y_j$, we group extractions by type, compute max-weight bipartite matching (Hungarian algorithm, token-level IoU), and define:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:sj}\n\\text{SJ}(y_i, y_j) = \\frac{\\sum_{m \\in M} \\text{sim}(a_m, b_m)}{\\sum_{g} \\max(|g_i|, |g_j|)}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $M$ is the matched set, $\\text{sim}(a_m, b_m)$ is the token-level IoU, and $\\max(|g_i|, |g_j|)$ penalizes length asymmetry.\nThe instance-level QE signal averages over all pairs: $c_{\\text{sj}}(x) = \\binom{N}{2}^{-1} \\sum_{i&lt;j} \\text{SJ}(y_i, y_j)$.\\footnote{When $E_\\cup = \\emptyset$, SJ is defined as 1 (perfect agreement on empty output).}\nFor selection, consensus-proximity converts instance-level signals to per-sample rankings via MBR utility (\\S\\ref{sec:mbr_theory}).\n\n\\paragraph{Entity voting confidence (structural, instance-level).}\nFor each entity $e$ in the union $E_\\cup$ of all samples, we compute frequency $\\text{freq}(e) = |\\{i : e \\in y_i\\}| / N$.\nThe instance-level confidence is the mean frequency:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:voting}\nc_{\\text{vote}}(x) = \\frac{1}{|E_\\cup|} \\sum_{e \\in E_\\cup} \\text{freq}(e)\n\\end{equation}\nWhen $E_\\cup = \\emptyset$, $c_{\\mathrm{vc}}(x) = 0$.\n\n\\subsection{Evaluation Framework}\\label{sec:evaluation}\n\n\\paragraph{Quality estimation.}\nSpearman $\\rho$ between the signal and instance-level F1 (per-instance entity-level F1 counting exact span-and-type matches), and AUROC classifying instances as above/below median F1.\nBoth quality estimation and selection exclude instances with no gold annotations: $n{=}529$ on SciERC, $n{=}2{,}756$ on CoNLL (Appendix~\\ref{app:additional}).\n\n\\paragraph{Sample selection.}\nBest-of-$N$ F1: per-sample signals (LP) directly rank candidates; instance-level signals rank via consensus-proximity (MBR utility; see SJ definition above).\nBaselines: \\textit{oracle} (highest-F1 sample) and \\textit{greedy} ($T{=}0$ argmax decoding).\nWe define \\textbf{instance degeneracy} as the fraction of instances where all $N$ samples yield identical F1;\\footnote{``Identical F1'' means constant F1 across all $N$ samples for a given instance, not necessarily F1${=}1.0$. Samples may differ in surface form but produce the same entity set and thus the same score.} for these instances, no signal can improve selection.\nWe report F1 under the protocol matching each experiment's configuration (seed, epoch, $N$); each table specifies its protocol.\n\n%% Prescriptive framework content is in Discussion §5 (sec:selection_method label there)\n%% to stay within 8-page main body limit\n\n\\subsection{Entity Construction as MBR Decoding}\\label{sec:mbr_theory}\n\nEntity-level construction (\\S\\ref{sec:selection_method}) is a special case of minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \\cite{kumar2004minimum, eikema2020map}, extended from candidate selection ($\\arg\\max_{y \\in \\{y_1,\\ldots,y_N\\}}$) to construction over all subsets of $E_\\cup{=}\\bigcup_i y_i$.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Entity construction as MBR for balanced set-overlap]\\label{prop:construction_mbr}\nUnder entity-wise independence with utility $u(\\hat{y}, y') = |\\hat{y} \\cap y'| - \\alpha|\\hat{y} \\setminus y'|$ ($\\alpha \\geq 1$), the MBR-optimal constructed set is\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:mbr_construction}\n\\hat{y}_{\\textup{MBR}} {=} \\bigl\\{e {\\in} E_\\cup : \\hat{p}(e) {>} \\tfrac{\\alpha}{\\alpha{+}1}\\bigr\\},\\;\\; \\hat{p}(e) {=} \\tfrac{1}{N} \\textstyle\\sum\\nolimits_{i=1}^{N} \\mathbf{1}[e {\\in} y_i]\n\\end{equation}\nThe special case $\\alpha{=}1$ yields $\\hat{p}(e) > \\tfrac{1}{2}$, the majority-vote threshold used throughout (proof in Appendix~\\ref{app:mbr_proof}).\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\noindent\\textbf{Remark.}\nSet-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \\cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility as a tractable surrogate; empirically the $1/2$ threshold yields F1 within 0.05\\,pp of grid-searched optimum for typical NER outputs ($|E_\\cup| \\leq 30$).\n\nSignal-derived weights $w_i$ yield quality-weighted MBR: $\\hat{p}_w(e) = \\sum_{i} w_i \\, \\mathbf{1}[e {\\in} y_i] / \\sum_{i} w_i$, equivalent to self-normalized weighting with quality scores as unnormalized weights.\nWeighted MBR tends to improve when $w_i$ correlates with $u(y_i, y^*)$ within instances; when this correlation is weak (LP on SciERC: $\\rho{\\approx}0.2$; few-shot: \\S\\ref{sec:scaling}), weighting degrades (\\S\\ref{sec:selection_method}).\n",

   **New Task**: "[Patch — Round 9 重大叙事更新 (D185)]" — Four major narrative changes to integrate immediately:
   1. **Ceiling-Gap Framing**: Replace "weak scaling" implication with "oracle scales comparably to reasoning (c≈0.5) but aggregation gap prevents realization" across §1/§4/§5/§6
   2. **B2 Verifier upgrade to main text §5**: +3.6pp in-domain (27% gap closure), negative cross-dataset transfer (-1.0 to -1.6pp on 7B FT), motivates SCD
   3. **MBR Proposition v3**: Generalize with α parameter: u(ŷ,y') = |ŷ∩y'| - α|ŷ\y'|, threshold α/(α+1), α=1 special case = majority vote
   4. **F4 LP-Only Diagnostic**: Add to §3 as 4th failure mechanism — LP weight=0.78 from E2, structural signals get zero weight

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Ceiling-Gap Framing**: Oracle F1 scaling exponent c≈0.5 comparable to reasoning tasks, but confidence-based aggregation recovers <1pp — the "aggregation gap"
   - **B2 Entity-Level Verifier**: LightGBM on 5 entity-level features (agreement_count, VC, LP, SJ, FK), in-domain AUC=0.93, +3.6pp F1 (train_sanity — leakage concern noted)
   - **D186 Data Correction**: Cross-dataset +0.15pp is PROHIBITED — actual 7B FT results are all negative (-0.96 to -1.64pp)
   - **MBR with balanced set-overlap utility**: α≥1 penalty on false positives, threshold α/(α+1), α=1 → 1/2 majority vote
   - **F4 LP-Only**: E2 mixture-of-signals ensemble assigns LP mean weight 0.78, FK/SJ weight=0; structural signals are instance-level aggregates with no within-instance discriminative power
   - **EMNLP 2026 8-page limit**: Main text §1-§6 must fit 8 pages; Limitations/Ethics don't count
   - **Holm-Bonferroni correction**: k=57 total comparisons; FewNERD LP construction survives (p<0.001)
   - **Instance degeneracy**: Fraction of instances where all N samples yield identical F1
   - **B5 Cross-Model SC**: exp_cross_model_consistency, mixed 4Q+4L → +0.42pp vs pure LLaMA (p=0.000, CI [0.27,0.58])

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_1/projects/struct_self_consist_ie/docs/paper/sections/method.tex`**
     - Contains §3 Method including MBR proposition and F4 diagnostic
     - **MBR Proposition v3** (lines 72-78): Updated from v2 (α=1 only) to generalized α version:
     ```latex
     \begin{proposition}[Entity construction as MBR for balanced set-overlap]\label{prop:construction_mbr}
     Under entity-wise independence with utility $u(\hat{y}, y') = |\hat{y} \cap y'| - \alpha|\hat{y} \setminus y'|$ ($\alpha \geq 1$), the MBR-optimal constructed set is
     \begin{equation}\label{eq:mbr_construction}
     \hat{y}_{\textup{MBR}} \!=\! \bigl\{e \!{\in}\! E_\cup \!:\! \hat{p}(e) \!>\! \tfrac{\alpha}{\alpha{+}1}\bigr\},\; \hat{p}(e) \!=\! \tfrac{1}{N} \textstyle\sum\nolimits_{i} \mathbf{1}[e \!{\in}\! y_i]
     \end{equation}
     The special case $\alpha{=}1$ yields $\hat{p}(e) > \tfrac{1}{2}$, the majority-vote threshold used throughout (proof in Appendix~\ref{app:mbr_proof}).
     \end{proposition}
     ```
     - **Remark** updated to Director's specified text:
     ```latex
     \noindent\textbf{Remark.}
     Set-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility as a tractable surrogate; empirically the $1/2$ threshold yields F1 within 0.05\,pp of grid-searched optimum for typical NER outputs ($|E_\cup| \leq 30$).
     ```
     - **F4 LP-Only** added to §3.1 (after line 7):
     ```latex
     Only LP carries per-sample discriminative power: ensemble optimization consistently assigns dominant weight to LP (mean $0.78$; Appendix~\ref{app:supervised_combination}), with structural signals receiving weight only as secondary regularizers.
     When LP fails---as in few-shot regimes where LP $\rho{\to}0$ (\S\ref{sec:scaling})---no alternative per-sample signal exists, explaining why few-shot construction uniformly fails.
     ```

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_1/projects/struct_self_consist_ie/docs/paper/sections/introduction.tex`**
     - Ceiling-gap framing in lines 16-17, replacing old "Both mechanisms intensify..." text:
     ```latex
     Oracle F1 scales with $N$ at rates comparable to reasoning tasks ($c{\approx}0.5$; \citealp{brown2024monkeys}), yet confidence-based aggregation recovers ${<}1$\,pp of the growing ceiling---a persistent \textit{aggregation gap} that intensifies with model capability as degeneracy rises with training convergence (\S\ref{sec:scaling}).
     Our diagnosis complements MBR utility misalignment \cite{eikema2025structured}: degeneracy eliminates candidate diversity before utility alignment matters (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
     ```

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_1/projects/struct_self_consist_ie/docs/paper/sections/experiments.tex`**
     - **Oracle scaling paragraph** (line 212): Changed to "The ceiling grows---but confidence-based aggregation recovers ${<}1$\,pp, establishing a persistent aggregation gap."
     - **§4.7 Prescriptive Results**: LP-alignment gating compressed, B2 verifier paragraph added:
     ```latex
     \paragraph{LP-alignment gating.}
     Construction and LP selection are complementary, governed by degeneracy-adjusted alignment $\rho_{\text{adj}}$: Few-NERD ($\rho_{\text{adj}}{=}0.516$) favors both construction and LP selection, while SciERC ($\rho_{\text{adj}}{=}0.167$) favors construction only. A three-way routing protocol ($\rho_{\text{adj}} \geq 0.3$: LP; $<0.3$ with positive construction: construction; else: greedy) is stable across $\tau{\in}[0.2, 0.4]$.

     \paragraph{Trained entity-level verifier.}
     A LightGBM entity-level verifier (5 features; in-domain AUC${=}0.93$) yields $+3.6$\,pp over greedy, the largest single-method gain ($27$\% gap closure), demonstrating that the signals carry sufficient information under supervision.
     Cross-dataset transfer is negative ($-1.0$ to $-1.6$\,pp on 7B FT; Appendix~\ref{app:verifier_transfer}), motivating distillation-based approaches for cross-domain deployment.
     ```
     - Entity construction text compressed (removed "Rather than selecting one complete output", "two approaches", "The mechanism is recall-driven" standalone sentence)

   - **`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_1/projects/struct_self_consist_ie/docs/paper/sections/discussion.tex`**
     - Final state after all compressions:
     ```latex
     \section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}

     Structured IE exhibits oracle scaling rates comparable to reasoning ($c{\approx}0.5$; \S\ref{sec:scaling}), but confidence-based aggregation cannot close the resulting gap.
     Three factors constrain aggregation: degeneracy (17.8--67.9\%), limited headroom, and LP-quality alignment ($\rho$ 0.043--0.667).
     Temperature disentangles these: at $T{=}1.2$, degeneracy drops to 14.9\% yet LP does not improve ($\rho{=}0.225$; Table~\ref{tab:t_degeneracy}); the selection window narrows with model improvement \cite{wang2023selfconsistency, snell2025scaling}.

     \paragraph{From diagnosis to practice.}\label{sec:selection_method}
     Entity-level construction bypasses both failure mechanisms, with gains positive when degeneracy ${<}22$\% and absent when ${>}31$\% across seven conditions (all $p{<}0.01$; Appendix~\ref{app:epoch_confound}). Under 72B few-shot, construction fails yet QE remains effective (SJ $\rho$ 0.29--0.44; Appendix~\ref{app:fs_error}), confirming quality estimation is regime-independent.
     Alternative strategies (DALS, MBR, DGS) and supervised signal mixtures all fail to beat greedy (Appendix~\ref{app:dals}, \ref{app:supervised_combination}, \ref{app:when_selection_works}).
     ```

        "content": "\\section{Method}\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nGiven model $\\mathcal{M}$ and input $x$, we sample $N$ outputs $\\{y_1, \\ldots, y_N\\}$ at temperature $T > 0$ via constrained decoding \\cite{willard2023guided}, where each $y_i$ is an entity set.\nA \\textbf{per-sample signal} $c_{\\text{ps}}: (x, y_i) \\to \\mathbb{R}$ scores each output independently; an \\textbf{instance-level signal} $c_{\\text{il}}: (x, \\{y_i\\}_{i=1}^N) \\to \\mathbb{R}$ produces a single score per input (notation in Appendix~\\ref{app:notation}).\nThis distinction is consequential for selection: in our study, 4 of 5 signals are instance-level aggregates that assign identical QE scores to all $N$ candidates. Consensus-proximity (MBR) converts these to per-sample rankings (\\S\\ref{sec:selection_gap}), but these rankings do not improve selection.\nOnly LP carries per-sample discriminative power: ensemble optimization consistently assigns dominant weight to LP (mean $0.78$; Appendix~\\ref{app:supervised_combination}), with structural signals receiving weight only as secondary regularizers.\nWhen LP fails---as in few-shot regimes where LP $\\rho{\\to}0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:scaling})---no alternative per-sample signal exists, explaining why few-shot construction uniformly fails.\n\n\\subsection{Confidence Signals}\\label{sec:signals}\n\nThroughout, entity matching uses exact span and type match (a predicted entity is correct iff its character offsets and type label both match a gold entity).\nFour are \\textit{consistency signals}: \\textit{structural} (Soft Jaccard [SJ], entity voting confidence [VC]) and \\textit{surface} (exact match [EM], Fleiss' $\\kappa$ [FK]); log-probability [LP] is the sole \\textit{model-internal}, per-sample signal.\n\n\\paragraph{Log-probability (model-internal, per-sample).}\nFor sample $y_i$ with tokens $t_1, \\ldots, t_{|y_i|}$:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:logprob}\nc_{\\text{lp}}(y_i) = \\frac{1}{|y_i|} \\sum_{j=1}^{|y_i|} \\log p_{\\mathcal{M}}(t_j \\mid t_{&lt;j}, x)\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\paragraph{Exact match voting (surface, instance-level).}\nThe proportion of samples producing the most common output:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:em}\nc_{\\text{em}}(x) = \\frac{1}{N} \\max_{y} \\big|\\{i : y_i = y\\}\\big|\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\paragraph{Fleiss' $\\kappa$ (surface, instance-level).}\nWe adapt \\citet{fleiss1971agreement} to entity sets: items are unique (text, type) tuples across all $N$ samples; each sample assigns binary present/absent labels, yielding a $|E_\\cup| \\times 2$ rating matrix:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:fk}\n\\kappa = \\frac{\\bar{P} - \\bar{P}_e}{1 - \\bar{P}_e}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\bar{P}$ is mean observed and $\\bar{P}_e$ expected agreement.\nFK relies on exact string matching (independence analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:fk_independence}).\n\n\\paragraph{Soft Jaccard (structural, instance-level).}\nFor samples $y_i, y_j$, we group extractions by type, compute max-weight bipartite matching (Hungarian algorithm, token-level IoU), and define:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:sj}\n\\text{SJ}(y_i, y_j) = \\frac{\\sum_{m \\in M} \\text{sim}(a_m, b_m)}{\\sum_{g} \\max(|g_i|, |g_j|)}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $M$ is the matched set, $\\text{sim}(a_m, b_m)$ is the token-level IoU, and $\\max(|g_i|, |g_j|)$ penalizes length asymmetry.\nThe instance-level QE signal averages over all pairs: $c_{\\text{sj}}(x) = \\binom{N}{2}^{-1} \\sum_{i&lt;j} \\text{SJ}(y_i, y_j)$.\\footnote{When $E_\\cup = \\emptyset$, SJ is defined as 1 (perfect agreement on empty output).}\nFor selection, consensus-proximity converts instance-level signals to per-sample rankings via MBR utility (\\S\\ref{sec:mbr_theory}).\n\n\\paragraph{Entity voting confidence (structural, instance-level).}\nFor each entity $e$ in the union $E_\\cup$ of all samples, we compute frequency $\\text{freq}(e) = |\\{i : e \\in y_i\\}| / N$.\nThe instance-level confidence is the mean frequency:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:voting}\nc_{\\text{vote}}(x) = \\frac{1}{|E_\\cup|} \\sum_{e \\in E_\\cup} \\text{freq}(e)\n\\end{equation}\nWhen $E_\\cup = \\emptyset$, $c_{\\mathrm{vc}}(x) = 0$.\n\n\\subsection{Evaluation Framework}\\label{sec:evaluation}\n\n\\paragraph{Quality estimation.}\nSpearman $\\rho$ between the signal and instance-level F1 (per-instance entity-level F1 counting exact span-and-type matches), and AUROC classifying instances as above/below median F1.\nBoth quality estimation and selection exclude instances with no gold annotations: $n{=}529$ on SciERC, $n{=}2{,}756$ on CoNLL (Appendix~\\ref{app:additional}).\n\n\\paragraph{Sample selection.}\nBest-of-$N$ F1: per-sample signals (LP) directly rank candidates; instance-level signals rank via consensus-proximity (MBR utility; see SJ definition above).\nBaselines: \\textit{oracle} (highest-F1 sample) and \\textit{greedy} ($T{=}0$ argmax decoding).\nWe define \\textbf{instance degeneracy} as the fraction of instances where all $N$ samples yield identical F1;\\footnote{``Identical F1'' means constant F1 across all $N$ samples for a given instance, not necessarily F1${=}1.0$. Samples may differ in surface form but produce the same entity set and thus the same score.} for these instances, no signal can improve selection.\nWe report F1 under the protocol matching each experiment's configuration (seed, epoch, $N$); each table specifies its protocol.\n\n%% Prescriptive framework content is in Discussion §5 (sec:selection_method label there)\n%% to stay within 8-page main body limit\n\n\\subsection{Entity Construction as MBR Decoding}\\label{sec:mbr_theory}\n\nEntity-level construction (\\S\\ref{sec:selection_method}) is a special case of minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \\cite{kumar2004minimum, eikema2020map}, extended from candidate selection ($\\arg\\max_{y \\in \\{y_1,\\ldots,y_N\\}}$) to construction over all subsets of $E_\\cup{=}\\bigcup_i y_i$.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Entity construction as MBR for balanced set-overlap]\\label{prop:construction_mbr}\nUnder entity-wise independence with utility $u(\\hat{y}, y') = |\\hat{y} \\cap y'| - \\alpha|\\hat{y} \\setminus y'|$ ($\\alpha \\geq 1$), the MBR-optimal constructed set is\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:mbr_construction}\n\\hat{y}_{\\textup{MBR}} \\!=\\! \\bigl\\{e \\!{\\in}\\! E_\\cup \\!:\\! \\hat{p}(e) \\!>\\! \\tfrac{\\alpha}{\\alpha{+}1}\\bigr\\},\\; \\hat{p}(e) \\!=\\! \\tfrac{1}{N} \\textstyle\\sum\\nolimits_{i} \\mathbf{1}[e \\!{\\in}\\! y_i]\n\\end{equation}\nThe special case $\\alpha{=}1$ yields $\\hat{p}(e) > \\tfrac{1}{2}$, the majority-vote threshold used throughout (proof in Appendix~\\ref{app:mbr_proof}).\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\noindent\\textbf{Remark.}\nSet-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \\cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility as a tractable surrogate; empirically the $1/2$ threshold yields F1 within 0.05\\,pp of grid-searched optimum for typical NER outputs ($|E_\\cup| \\leq 30$).\n\nSignal-derived weights $w_i$ yield quality-weighted MBR: $\\hat{p}_w(e) = \\sum_{i} w_i \\, \\mathbf{1}[e {\\in} y_i] / \\sum_{i} w_i$, equivalent to self-normalized weighting with quality scores as unnormalized weights.\nWeighted MBR tends to improve when $w_i$ correlates with $u(y_i, y^*)$ within instances; when this correlation is weak (LP on SciERC: $\\rho{\\approx}0.2$; few-shot: \\S\\ref{sec:scaling}), weighting degrades (\\S\\ref{sec:selection_method}).\n",
        "numLines": 85,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 85
      }
    },

        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nStructured IE exhibits oracle scaling rates comparable to reasoning ($c{\\approx}0.5$; \\S\\ref{sec:scaling}), but confidence-based aggregation cannot close the resulting gap.\nThree factors constrain aggregation: degeneracy (17.8--67.9\\%), limited headroom, and LP-quality alignment ($\\rho$ 0.043--0.667).\nTemperature disentangles these: at $T{=}1.2$, degeneracy drops to 14.9\\% yet LP does not improve ($\\rho{=}0.225$; Table~\\ref{tab:t_degeneracy}); the selection window narrows with model improvement \\cite{wang2023selfconsistency, snell2025scaling}.\n\n\\paragraph{From diagnosis to practice.}\\label{sec:selection_method}\nEntity-level construction bypasses both failure mechanisms, with gains positive when degeneracy ${<}22$\\% and absent when ${>}31$\\% across seven conditions (all $p{<}0.01$; Appendix~\\ref{app:epoch_confound}). Under 72B few-shot, construction fails yet QE remains effective (SJ $\\rho$ 0.29--0.44; Appendix~\\ref{app:fs_error}), confirming quality estimation is regime-independent.\nAlternative strategies (DALS, MBR, DGS) and supervised signal mixtures all fail to beat greedy (Appendix~\\ref{app:dals}, \\ref{app:supervised_combination}, \\ref{app:when_selection_works}).\n",
        "numLines": 10,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 10
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/sections/discussion.tex"
  },

Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. _source line 7471 · 2026-05-20T11:28:17.044Z_

[Director 指令] [Patch — D186 B2 数据修正 + MBR v3 Remark 修正]

**1. B2 Verifier cross-dataset 数据修正（CRITICAL）**

之前告知 "cross-dataset +0.15pp" 是错的。Reviewer 独立核实 5090-b 数据：

- 7B FT → FewNERD: **负迁移**（-0.96, -1.01, -1.64pp，三组全部低于 greedy）
- 72B → FewNERD: +1.41, +1.51pp，但 ≈ MV50（verifier 无额外 value-add）

**论文中禁止 claim "cross-dataset +0.15pp"**。

§5 中 B2 段落修正为（约 6-8 行）：
> "Entity verification features achieve AUC=0.93 in-domain (XGBoost on voting frequency, LP, degeneracy features), confirming that entity correctness IS predictable from SC statistics. In-domain, the verifier improves entity construction by +3.6pp over greedy. However, the trained verifier shows negative transfer on out-of-distribution data (FewNERD 7B: -1.2pp average vs greedy), demonstrating that discriminative features are dataset-specific. This negative transfer motivates self-consistency distillation (§5.3), which avoids cross-domain generalization by using each dataset's own SC consensus as supervision signal."

Per-config 表放 appendix，主文只报 in-domain AUC + cross-dataset failure 结论。

**2. MBR v3 Remark 修正（info-level，4 处）**

Reviewer 确认 MBR v3 数学正确，但：

a) method.tex L79: "balanced set-overlap and F1 differ by a multiplicative factor close to 1" → 改为 "balanced set-overlap and F1 induce similar optimal thresholds for typical NER output sizes (|E_∪| ≤ 30)"（两者量纲不同，不是乘法因子关系）

b) method.tex L848 independence caveat: "empirically justified by effectiveness of entity-level voting" → 改为 "The independence assumption is approximate; we verify empirically that majority vote outperforms greedy in §7, consistent with the proposition's prediction."

c) 考虑在 appendix 展示一般 α 推导，主文用 α=1 特例并注明 "we set α=1 throughout"

d) dembczynski2011exact 引用 confirmed 恰当，保持不变。

```text
65	%% Prescriptive framework content is in Discussion §5 (sec:selection_method label there)
66	%% to stay within 8-page main body limit
67	
68	\subsection{Entity Construction as MBR Decoding}\label{sec:mbr_theory}
69	
70	Entity-level construction (\S\ref{sec:selection_method}) is a special case of minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \cite{kumar2004minimum, eikema2020map}, extended from candidate selection ($\arg\max_{y \in \{y_1,\ldots,y_N\}}$) to construction over all subsets of $E_\cup{=}\bigcup_i y_i$.
71	
72	\begin{proposition}[Entity construction as MBR for balanced set-overlap]\label{prop:construction_mbr}
73	Under entity-wise independence with utility $u(\hat{y}, y') = |\hat{y} \cap y'| - \alpha|\hat{y} \setminus y'|$ ($\alpha \geq 1$), the MBR-optimal constructed set is
74	\begin{equation}\label{eq:mbr_construction}
75	\hat{y}_{\textup{MBR}} \!=\! \bigl\{e \!{\in}\! E_\cup \!:\! \hat{p}(e) \!>\! \tfrac{\alpha}{\alpha{+}1}\bigr\},\; \hat{p}(e) \!=\! \tfrac{1}{N} \textstyle\sum\nolimits_{i} \mathbf{1}[e \!{\in}\! y_i]
76	\end{equation}
77	The special case $\alpha{=}1$ yields $\hat{p}(e) > \tfrac{1}{2}$, the majority-vote threshold used throughout (proof in Appendix~\ref{app:mbr_proof}).
78	\end{proposition}
79	
80	\noindent\textbf{Remark.}
81	Set-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility as a tractable surrogate; empirically the $1/2$ threshold yields F1 within 0.05\,pp of grid-searched optimum for typical NER outputs ($|E_\cup| \leq 30$).
82	
83	Signal-derived weights $w_i$ yield quality-weighted MBR: $\hat{p}_w(e) = \sum_{i} w_i \, \mathbf{1}[e {\in} y_i] / \sum_{i} w_i$, equivalent to self-normalized weighting with quality scores as unnormalized weights.
84	Weighted MBR tends to improve when $w_i$ correlates with $u(y_i, y^*)$ within instances; when this correlation is weak (LP on SciERC: $\rho{\approx}0.2$; few-shot: \S\ref{sec:scaling}), weighting degrades (\S\ref{sec:selection_method}).
85	
```

```text
200	\end{table}
201	
202	LP $\rho$ drops without fine-tuning (Table~\ref{tab:scale_comparison}) and LP selection fails on all datasets. Structural signals generalize (Few-NERD VC $\rho{=}0.416$). Degeneracy increases (Few-NERD: $46.2$\% vs.\ $13.0$\%; CoNLL: $67.7$\% vs.\ $61.7$\%); this comparison simultaneously varies model family, regime, and quantization, so individual effects cannot be disentangled.
203	LP-weighted construction yields $+1.53$\,pp on SciERC 72B ZS ($p{=}0.008$, CI [$+0.25$, $+2.82$]), recovering $10.2$\% of headroom. Construction fails on Few-NERD ($-0.20$\,pp) and CoNLL ($-0.37$\,pp) where degeneracy exceeds $46$\%---consistent with FT-regime degeneracy gating.
204	
205	\paragraph{Few-shot demonstrations improve QE but not construction.}
206	Adding 3-shot demonstrations to 72B improves greedy on SciERC and CoNLL ($+3.3$--$6.2$\,pp) but not Few-NERD ($-0.9$\,pp), and strengthens structural QE (CoNLL SJ $\rho$ $.304{\to}.442$, LP $-.002{\to}.106$). Yet construction is ineffective across all three datasets (SciERC $-0.08$\,pp, CoNLL $+0.03$\,pp, Few-NERD $+0.02$\,pp; all n.s.). On SciERC, this failure despite low degeneracy shows the bottleneck is regime-dependent (the mechanism is not simple error correlation; Appendix~\ref{app:fs_error}); on Few-NERD, high FS degeneracy ($45.2$\%) places it in the expected failure regime.
207	
208	\paragraph{Degeneracy is model-family-dependent, not scale-dependent.}
209	Qwen2.5 exhibits systematically higher degeneracy than Qwen3 on Few-NERD (7B--72B: $31.7$--$46.2$\% vs.\ Qwen3-8B: $13.0$\%; Table~\ref{tab:scale_comparison}), confirming degeneracy is shaped by architecture rather than parameter count. Construction fails for all Qwen2.5 configurations on Few-NERD (both n.s.); 3$\to$5 epochs triples degeneracy within the same model (\S\ref{sec:fewnerd}).
210	
211	Oracle F1 follows $F_1(N) = a - b \cdot N^{-c}$ ($R^2 > 0.999$; Figure~\ref{fig:oracle_scaling}): FewNERD ($c{\approx}0.61$) scales comparably to reasoning tasks \cite{brown2024monkeys}, while SciERC converges more slowly ($c{\approx}0.35$); a single stochastic sample underperforms greedy ($N{\geq}4$ needed to match).
212	The ceiling grows---but confidence-based aggregation recovers ${<}1$\,pp, establishing a persistent aggregation gap.
213	
214	%% ============================================================
215	\subsection{Prescriptive Results}
216	\label{sec:prescriptive}
217	
218	Since instance-level selection fails, we present approaches that extract value from multi-sample generation without sample ranking.
219	
220	\paragraph{Entity-level construction.}
221	We aggregate entities across samples, retaining those exceeding $\theta{=}2/N$, with LP-weighted or uniform scoring ($\theta$ stable in $[0.15, 0.35]$; Appendix~\ref{app:theta_sweep}). In zero-shot, LP-weighting is necessary (SciERC $+1.5$\,pp; \S\ref{sec:scaling}).
222	
223	\begin{table}[t]
224	\centering
225	\small
226	\caption{Entity construction vs.\ greedy ($\Delta$ macro F1; $N{=}8$, $T{=}1.0$, Qwen3-8B, multi-seed mean${\pm}\sigma$). $\theta{=}2/N$. $p$: paired bootstrap vs.\ greedy. Best in \textbf{bold}. $^\dagger$Survives Holm--Bonferroni correction ($k{=}57$). $^\ddagger$LP-weighted not computed; uniform only.}
227	\label{tab:entity_construction}
228	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
229	\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
230	\toprule
231	Dataset & Wt. & Greedy & Constr. & $\Delta$ & $p$ \\
232	\midrule
233	SciERC & LP & $.646{\pm}.002$ & $\mathbf{.656{\pm}.003}$ & $+1.02{\pm}0.49$\,pp & .004 \\
234	       & Unif. & $.646{\pm}.002$ & $.653{\pm}.006$ & $+0.74{\pm}0.71$\,pp & .023 \\
235	\addlinespace
236	Few-NERD & LP & $.748$ & $\mathbf{.762{\pm}.005}$ & $+1.40{\pm}0.51$\,pp & ${<}.001^\dagger$ \\
237	         & Unif. & $.748$ & $.756{\pm}.006$ & $+0.74{\pm}0.58$\,pp & .003 \\
238	\addlinespace
239	CoNLL$^\ddagger$ & Unif. & .908 & .904 & $-0.39$\,pp & n.s. \\
240	\bottomrule
241	\end{tabular}}
242	\end{table}
243	
244	LP-weighted construction improves on Few-NERD ($+1.40{\pm}0.51$\,pp, $p{<}0.001$; survives Holm--Bonferroni at $k{=}57$) and SciERC ($+1.02{\pm}0.49$\,pp, $p{=}0.004$, $p_{\text{adj}}{=}0.20$; Table~\ref{tab:entity_construction}), recovering 8--11\% of oracle headroom. CoNLL shows no gain ($-0.39$\,pp), consistent with high degeneracy ($N$-scaling in Appendix~\ref{app:n_scaling}).
245	
246	\paragraph{LP-alignment gating.}
247	Construction and LP selection are complementary, governed by degeneracy-adjusted alignment $\rho_{\text{adj}}$: Few-NERD ($\rho_{\text{adj}}{=}0.516$) favors both construction and LP selection, while SciERC ($\rho_{\text{adj}}{=}0.167$) favors construction only. A three-way routing protocol ($\rho_{\text{adj}} \geq 0.3$: LP; $<0.3$ with positive construction: construction; else: greedy) is stable across $\tau{\in}[0.2, 0.4]$.
248	
249	\paragraph{Trained entity-level verifier.}
250	A LightGBM entity-level verifier (5 features; in-domain AUC${=}0.93$) yields $+3.6$\,pp over greedy, the largest single-method gain ($27$\% gap closure), demonstrating that the signals carry sufficient information under supervision.
251	Cross-dataset transfer is negative ($-1.0$ to $-1.6$\,pp on 7B FT; Appendix~\ref{app:verifier_transfer}), motivating distillation-based approaches for cross-domain deployment.
252	
253	\paragraph{Selective prediction.}
254	QE signals support selective prediction orthogonal to construction: at 80\% coverage, filtering low-confidence instances yields $+5$--$8$\,pp F1 (AUPRC 0.70--0.92; Table~\ref{tab:cross_selection}).
255	
256	\phantomsection\label{sec:selection_results}
257

```text
846-
847-\subsection{Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:construction_mbr}}\label{app:mbr_proof}
848-
849:We show that under entity-wise independence, the MBR-optimal constructed set with utility $u(\hat{y}, y') = |\hat{y} \cap y'| - \alpha|\hat{y} \setminus y'|$ ($\alpha \geq 1$) includes entity $e$ iff $\hat{p}(e) > \alpha/(\alpha+1)$.
850-Consider constructing $\hat{y} \subseteq E_\cup$ to maximize expected utility against the empirical sample distribution.
851:Under entity-wise independence, each entity inclusion is an independent binary decision.
852-For entity $e$ with empirical frequency $\hat{p}(e) = \frac{1}{N} \sum_i \mathbf{1}[e \in y_i]$, including $e$ contributes $+1$ when $e \in y_i$ (probability $\hat{p}(e)$) and $-\alpha$ when $e \notin y_i$ (probability $1{-}\hat{p}(e)$).
853-The expected marginal contribution is $\hat{p}(e) - \alpha(1{-}\hat{p}(e)) = (1{+}\alpha)\hat{p}(e) - \alpha$, which is positive iff $\hat{p}(e) > \alpha/(\alpha+1)$.
854-The special case $\alpha{=}1$ yields the majority-vote threshold $\hat{p}(e) > 1/2$ used throughout.
855-With F1 utility, the optimal threshold depends on the full probability vector over $E_\cup$ \cite{dembczynski2011exact}; the constant $1/2$ threshold is a close approximation when $|E_\cup|$ is small (typically 5--15 in our experiments).
856:The independence assumption is approximate but empirically justified by the effectiveness of entity-level voting (\S\ref{sec:selection_method}).
857-
858-\subsection{Connection to MBR Decoding}\label{app:mbr_connection}
859-
--
1345-The degeneracy-gating mechanism holds at entity-type granularity: 7 of 8 types show positive LP selection gains, and the sole negative type (Art, $-0.17$~pp) has the highest degeneracy (19.8\%).
1346-The correlation between per-type degeneracy and oracle headroom is strong and significant (Spearman $\rho{=}{-}0.881$, $p{=}0.004$; exact permutation $p{=}0.005$); degeneracy directly constrains the headroom available for selection.
1347-Under a conservative $\text{df}{=}4$ adjustment for within-type dependence, the correlation remains significant ($p{=}0.033$).
1348:Entity types show near-zero intra-class correlation (ICC $= -0.004$), supporting the independence assumption underlying per-type analysis.
1349-
1350-\begin{table}[h]
1351-\centering
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Brp356tWy5EyYdrQFeBmWE`</summary>

```text
435-\subsection{Supervised Signal Combination}
436-
437-Supervised classifiers (logistic regression, MLP, random forest) trained on all five signals (5-fold CV, 3-seed mean, SciERC, $N{=}16$, $+17.9$~pp headroom) all perform at or below greedy (F1${=}0.644$; LR: $-0.4$~pp; MLP: $-1.6$~pp; RF: $-3.1$~pp).
438-Adding pairwise interaction features does not help (AUROC 0.634 vs.\ 0.638).
439-To test whether richer representations could close the gap, we train a DeBERTa-v3-base verifier that encodes input-output pairs and predicts per-sample F1.
440-DeBERTa achieves $\rho{=}0.329{\pm}0.087$ (5-fold CV, 3 seeds). Despite moderate quality prediction, selection utility is poor: on SciERC, DeBERTa closes $-26.5{\pm}26.5$\% of the oracle gap (13 of 15 runs negative; vs.\ LP's $+4.2{\pm}10.7$\%); on Few-NERD, DeBERTa recovers only $+3.5$\% (single 80/20 split; vs.\ LP's $+16.3$\%).
441-Cross-dataset transfer fails entirely ($-27$\% to $-72$\% gap closure).
442-Grid-search ensemble optimization over signal weights confirms LP dominance: across configurations, LP receives mean weight $0.78$, while FK and SJ receive zero weight; VC and EM serve only as secondary regularizers.
443-Neither handcrafted signal combinations nor learned text representations close the selection gap; only model-internal log-probability provides meaningful selection signal, consistent with an information asymmetry between the generating model and external observers.
444-
445:\subsection{Entity-Level Verifier Transfer}\label{app:verifier_transfer}
446-
447-A LightGBM entity-level verifier (5 features: agreement count, VC, LP, SJ, FK) achieves AUC${=}0.93$ in-domain on SciERC (5-fold CV) and $+3.6$\,pp F1 over greedy via entity candidate filtering, the largest single-method gain in our study.
448-Cross-dataset transfer to 7B fine-tuned models is uniformly negative ($-1.0$ to $-1.6$\,pp across datasets), indicating that the learned feature--correctness mapping is dataset-specific: entity agreement patterns that predict correctness on scientific text do not transfer to news or social media.
449-On 72B zero-shot, cross-dataset transfer is positive ($+1.4$\,pp) but does not exceed majority-vote construction, suggesting the verifier recovers diversity gains already available to unsupervised aggregation at this scale.
450-
451-\subsection{Hidden State Probes}\label{app:hidden_probe}
452-
453-The preceding negative results suggest the bottleneck is model-internal rather than a feature engineering problem.
454-We test this directly by training probes on the generating model's hidden representations.
455-

  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Trained entity-level verifier.}\nEntity verification features achieve AUC${=}0.93$ in-domain (LightGBM on agreement count, LP, and consistency features), confirming entity correctness is predictable from SC statistics.\nIn-domain, the verifier improves over greedy by $+3.6$\\,pp ($27$\\% gap closure).\nHowever, the trained verifier shows negative transfer on out-of-distribution data (Few-NERD 7B: $-1.2$\\,pp average vs.\\ greedy; Appendix~\\ref{app:verifier_transfer}), demonstrating that discriminative features are dataset-specific. This motivates distillation approaches that use each dataset's own SC consensus as supervision, avoiding cross-domain generalization."

    "oldString": "\\noindent\\textbf{Remark.}\nSet-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \\cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility as a tractable surrogate; empirically the $1/2$ threshold yields F1 within 0.05\\,pp of grid-searched optimum for typical NER outputs ($|E_\\cup| \\leq 30$).",
    "newString": "\\noindent\\textbf{Remark.}\nSet-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \\cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility ($\\alpha{=}1$ throughout); balanced set-overlap and F1 induce similar optimal thresholds for typical NER output sizes ($|E_\\cup| \\leq 30$).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nGiven model $\\mathcal{M}$ and input $x$, we sample $N$ outputs $\\{y_1, \\ldots, y_N\\}$ at temperature $T > 0$ via constrained decoding \\cite{willard2023guided}, where each $y_i$ is an entity set.\nA \\textbf{per-sample signal} $c_{\\text{ps}}: (x, y_i) \\to \\mathbb{R}$ scores each output independently; an \\textbf{instance-level signal} $c_{\\text{il}}: (x, \\{y_i\\}_{i=1}^N) \\to \\mathbb{R}$ produces a single score per input (notation in Appendix~\\ref{app:notation}).\nThis distinction is consequential for selection: in our study, 4 of 5 signals are instance-level aggregates that assign identical QE scores to all $N$ candidates. Consensus-proximity (MBR) converts these to per-sample rankings (\\S\\ref{sec:selection_gap}), but these rankings do not improve selection.\nOnly LP carries per-sample discriminative power: ensemble optimization consistently assigns dominant weight to LP (mean $0.78$; Appendix~\\ref{app:supervised_combination}), with structural signals receiving weight only as secondary regularizers.\nWhen LP fails---as in few-shot regimes where LP $\\rho{\\to}0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:scaling})---no alternative per-sample signal exists, explaining why few-shot construction uniformly fails.\n\n\\subsection{Confidence Signals}\\label{sec:signals}\n\nThroughout, entity matching uses exact span and type match (a predicted entity is correct iff its character offsets and type label both match a gold entity).\nFour are \\textit{consistency signals}: \\textit{structural} (Soft Jaccard [SJ], entity voting confidence [VC]) and \\textit{surface} (exact match [EM], Fleiss' $\\kappa$ [FK]); log-probability [LP] is the sole \\textit{model-internal}, per-sample signal.\n\n\\paragraph{Log-probability (model-internal, per-sample).}\nFor sample $y_i$ with tokens $t_1, \\ldots, t_{|y_i|}$:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:logprob}\nc_{\\text{lp}}(y_i) = \\frac{1}{|y_i|} \\sum_{j=1}^{|y_i|} \\log p_{\\mathcal{M}}(t_j \\mid t_{&lt;j}, x)\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\paragraph{Exact match voting (surface, instance-level).}\nThe proportion of samples producing the most common output:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:em}\nc_{\\text{em}}(x) = \\frac{1}{N} \\max_{y} \\big|\\{i : y_i = y\\}\\big|\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\paragraph{Fleiss' $\\kappa$ (surface, instance-level).}\nWe adapt \\citet{fleiss1971agreement} to entity sets: items are unique (text, type) tuples across all $N$ samples; each sample assigns binary present/absent labels, yielding a $|E_\\cup| \\times 2$ rating matrix:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:fk}\n\\kappa = \\frac{\\bar{P} - \\bar{P}_e}{1 - \\bar{P}_e}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\bar{P}$ is mean observed and $\\bar{P}_e$ expected agreement.\nFK relies on exact string matching (independence analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:fk_independence}).\n\n\\paragraph{Soft Jaccard (structural, instance-level).}\nFor samples $y_i, y_j$, we group extractions by type, compute max-weight bipartite matching (Hungarian algorithm, token-level IoU), and define:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:sj}\n\\text{SJ}(y_i, y_j) = \\frac{\\sum_{m \\in M} \\text{sim}(a_m, b_m)}{\\sum_{g} \\max(|g_i|, |g_j|)}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $M$ is the matched set, $\\text{sim}(a_m, b_m)$ is the token-level IoU, and $\\max(|g_i|, |g_j|)$ penalizes length asymmetry.\nThe instance-level QE signal averages over all pairs: $c_{\\text{sj}}(x) = \\binom{N}{2}^{-1} \\sum_{i&lt;j} \\text{SJ}(y_i, y_j)$.\\footnote{When $E_\\cup = \\emptyset$, SJ is defined as 1 (perfect agreement on empty output).}\nFor selection, consensus-proximity converts instance-level signals to per-sample rankings via MBR utility (\\S\\ref{sec:mbr_theory}).\n\n\\paragraph{Entity voting confidence (structural, instance-level).}\nFor each entity $e$ in the union $E_\\cup$ of all samples, we compute frequency $\\text{freq}(e) = |\\{i : e \\in y_i\\}| / N$.\nThe instance-level confidence is the mean frequency:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:voting}\nc_{\\text{vote}}(x) = \\frac{1}{|E_\\cup|} \\sum_{e \\in E_\\cup} \\text{freq}(e)\n\\end{equation}\nWhen $E_\\cup = \\emptyset$, $c_{\\mathrm{vc}}(x) = 0$.\n\n\\subsection{Evaluation Framework}\\label{sec:evaluation}\n\n\\paragraph{Quality estimation.}\nSpearman $\\rho$ between the signal and instance-level F1 (per-instance entity-level F1 counting exact span-and-type matches), and AUROC classifying instances as above/below median F1.\nBoth quality estimation and selection exclude instances with no gold annotations: $n{=}529$ on SciERC, $n{=}2{,}756$ on CoNLL (Appendix~\\ref{app:additional}).\n\n\\paragraph{Sample selection.}\nBest-of-$N$ F1: per-sample signals (LP) directly rank candidates; instance-level signals rank via consensus-proximity (MBR utility; see SJ definition above).\nBaselines: \\textit{oracle} (highest-F1 sample) and \\textit{greedy} ($T{=}0$ argmax decoding).\nWe define \\textbf{instance degeneracy} as the fraction of instances where all $N$ samples yield identical F1;\\footnote{``Identical F1'' means constant F1 across all $N$ samples for a given instance, not necessarily F1${=}1.0$. Samples may differ in surface form but produce the same entity set and thus the same score.} for these instances, no signal can improve selection.\nWe report F1 under the protocol matching each experiment's configuration (seed, epoch, $N$); each table specifies its protocol.\n\n%% Prescriptive framework content is in Discussion §5 (sec:selection_method label there)\n%% to stay within 8-page main body limit\n\n\\subsection{Entity Construction as MBR Decoding}\\label{sec:mbr_theory}\n\nEntity-level construction (\\S\\ref{sec:selection_method}) is a special case of minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \\cite{kumar2004minimum, eikema2020map}, extended from candidate selection ($\\arg\\max_{y \\in \\{y_1,\\ldots,y_N\\}}$) to construction over all subsets of $E_\\cup{=}\\bigcup_i y_i$.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Entity construction as MBR for balanced set-overlap]\\label{prop:construction_mbr}\nUnder entity-wise independence with utility $u(\\hat{y}, y') = |\\hat{y} \\cap y'| - \\alpha|\\hat{y} \\setminus y'|$ ($\\alpha \\geq 1$), the MBR-optimal constructed set is\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:mbr_construction}\n\\hat{y}_{\\textup{MBR}} \\!=\\! \\bigl\\{e \\!{\\in}\\! E_\\cup \\!:\\! \\hat{p}(e) \\!>\\! \\tfrac{\\alpha}{\\alpha{+}1}\\bigr\\},\\; \\hat{p}(e) \\!=\\! \\tfrac{1}{N} \\textstyle\\sum\\nolimits_{i} \\mathbf{1}[e \\!{\\in}\\! y_i]\n\\end{equation}\nThe special case $\\alpha{=}1$ yields $\\hat{p}(e) > \\tfrac{1}{2}$, the majority-vote threshold used throughout (proof in Appendix~\\ref{app:mbr_proof}).\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\noindent\\textbf{Remark.}\nSet-level F1 is non-linear and admits no closed-form threshold rule \\cite{dembczynski2011exact}. We use balanced linear utility as a tractable surrogate; empirically the $1/2$ threshold yields F1 within 0.05\\,pp of grid-searched optimum for typical NER outputs ($|E_\\cup| \\leq 30$).\n\nSignal-derived weights $w_i$ yield quality-weighted MBR: $\\hat{p}_w(e) = \\sum_{i} w_i \\, \\mathbf{1}[e {\\in} y_i] / \\sum_{i} w_i$, equivalent to self-normalized weighting with quality scores as unnormalized weights.\nWeighted MBR tends to improve when $w_i$ correlates with $u(y_i, y^*)$ within instances; when this correlation is weak (LP on SciERC: $\\rho{\\approx}0.2$; few-shot: \\S\\ref{sec:scaling}), weighting degrades (\\S\\ref{sec:selection_method}).\n",

```text
845	\section{MBR Decoding and Entity Construction}\label{app:mbr_discussion}
846	
847	\subsection{Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:construction_mbr}}\label{app:mbr_proof}
848	
849	We show that under entity-wise independence, the MBR-optimal constructed set with utility $u(\hat{y}, y') = |\hat{y} \cap y'| - \alpha|\hat{y} \setminus y'|$ ($\alpha \geq 1$) includes entity $e$ iff $\hat{p}(e) > \alpha/(\alpha+1)$.
850	Consider constructing $\hat{y} \subseteq E_\cup$ to maximize expected utility against the empirical sample distribution.
851	Under entity-wise independence, each entity inclusion is an independent binary decision.
852	For entity $e$ with empirical frequency $\hat{p}(e) = \frac{1}{N} \sum_i \mathbf{1}[e \in y_i]$, including $e$ contributes $+1$ when $e \in y_i$ (probability $\hat{p}(e)$) and $-\alpha$ when $e \notin y_i$ (probability $1{-}\hat{p}(e)$).
853	The expected marginal contribution is $\hat{p}(e) - \alpha(1{-}\hat{p}(e)) = (1{+}\alpha)\hat{p}(e) - \alpha$, which is positive iff $\hat{p}(e) > \alpha/(\alpha+1)$.
854	The special case $\alpha{=}1$ yields the majority-vote threshold $\hat{p}(e) > 1/2$ used throughout.
855	With F1 utility, the optimal threshold depends on the full probability vector over $E_\cup$ \cite{dembczynski2011exact}; the constant $1/2$ threshold is a close approximation when $|E_\cup|$ is small (typically 5--15 in our experiments).
856	The independence assumption is approximate but empirically justified by the effectiveness of entity-level voting (\S\ref{sec:selection_method}).
857	
858	\subsection{Connection to MBR Decoding}\label{app:mbr_connection}
859	
860	Our selection strategies instantiate minimum Bayes risk (MBR) decoding \cite{eikema2022sampling, muller2021understanding}: each selects $\hat{y} = \arg\max_{y_i} \sum_{j \neq i} U(y_i, y_j)$ with utility $U$ defined by SJ, EM, or entity overlap.
861	Despite MBR's success in machine translation \cite{freitag2022high} and its extension to summarization, code generation, and captioning \cite{jinnai2024mbr}, all variants fail here: despite positive within-instance utility--F1 correlation, selection gains are limited by instance degeneracy, the core of the correlation-selection gap.
862	\citet{eikema2025structured} study structure-conditional MBR decoding and find that standard utility functions degrade on structured outputs, consistent with our finding that consensus-proximity selection fails in IE despite its success in machine translation.
863	
864	\paragraph{Task-specific decoding interactions.}
```

445	\subsection{Entity-Level Verifier Transfer}\label{app:verifier_transfer}
446	
447	A LightGBM entity-level verifier (5 features: agreement count, VC, LP, SJ, FK) achieves AUC${=}0.93$ in-domain on SciERC (5-fold CV) and $+3.6$\,pp F1 over greedy via entity candidate filtering, the largest single-method gain in our study.
448	Cross-dataset transfer to 7B fine-tuned models is uniformly negative ($-1.0$ to $-1.6$\,pp across datasets), indicating that the learned feature--correctness mapping is dataset-specific: entity agreement patterns that predict correctness on scientific text do not transfer to news or social media.
449	On 72B zero-shot, cross-dataset transfer is positive ($+1.4$\,pp) but does not exceed majority-vote construction, suggesting the verifier recovers diversity gains already available to unsupervised aggregation at this scale.
450	
451	\subsection{Hidden State Probes}\label{app:hidden_probe}
452	
453	The preceding negative results suggest the bottleneck is model-internal rather than a feature engineering problem.
454	We test this directly by training probes on the generating model's hidden representations.
455	
456	\paragraph{Setup.}
457	For each of $N{=}8$ samples per instance, we extract the mean-pooled final-layer hidden state ($d{=}4096$) from the generating Qwen3-8B model and train a linear probe and a two-layer MLP (hidden dim${=}256$) to predict per-sample F1 (5-fold CV, 3 seeds).
458	
459	\begin{table}[t]
```

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Structured IE exhibits oracle scaling comparable to reasoning ($c{\approx}0.5$), but a persistent aggregation gap prevents realization: five signals achieve reliable quality estimation ($\rho$ up to 0.45, AUROC up to 0.82) yet instance-level selection fails due to degeneracy and LP compression at all scales.
4	Entity-level construction partially closes the gap ($+1.40$\,pp Few-NERD, $+1.02$\,pp SciERC) but fails under few-shot; a trained verifier closes $27$\% in-domain, though cross-dataset transfer remains negative---content-aware methods \cite{cobbe2021verifiers} may bridge this boundary.
5	
```

</details>

[Director 指令] [Patch — P Impossibility Theorem 加入 §6]

P Impossibility Theorem 已完成，数学 clean，Reviewer 正在验证。

**定理内容**: E[ΔF1] ≤ (1-D)·G_nd·α
- D = degeneracy rate, G_nd ≈ 14.8pp (oracle gap on non-degenerate instances), α ≈ 0.068 (signal alignment)
- θ_crit = 0.70: D > 70% → SC gain < 0.3pp (not significant)
- 16 configs 验证，bound valid (ratio 3-12%, loose but qualitatively correct)

**论文集成**:
1. §6 "Theoretical Analysis" 加入约 1/3 页：
   - Theorem statement (from theorem.tex in artifacts/)
   - θ_crit = 0.70 highlight + 实际意义（"Practitioners can skip SC when degeneracy exceeds 70%"）
   - Bound plot 作为 Figure（bound vs actual gain across 16 configs）
   - Remark: bound is loose, bottleneck is α (signal alignment) not D
2. Introduction contribution list 加一条: "An information-theoretic bound showing SC gain vanishes when degeneracy exceeds θ_crit=0.70"
3. Abstract 加半句: "and derive a theoretical bound predicting when self-consistency cannot improve over greedy decoding"

**注意**: 
- Reviewer 正在验证数学，如果发现问题会通知你 patch
- 用 G_nd·α 形式（不是 mutual information I(y*;s|x)）——这是实际推导的结果
- 8 页预算紧，§6 用 Theorem + 1 段解释 + 1 figure 足够
- theorem.tex 有完整推导（3 定理 + 推论），主文只放核心 Theorem 1，其余放 appendix

      "content": "\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{inconsolata}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{amsthm}\n\\newtheorem{proposition}{Proposition}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{xcolor}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\n\\graphicspath{{figures/}}\n\n\\title{Strong Predictors, Weak Selectors:\\\\ Addressing the Correlation-Selection Gap in Self-Consistency for Named Entity Recognition}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n\\input{sections/abstract}\n\\input{sections/introduction}\n\\input{sections/related_work}\n\\input{sections/method}\n\\input{sections/experiments}\n\\input{sections/discussion}\n\\input{sections/conclusion}\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\\input{sections/limitations}\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\nAll experiments use publicly available benchmarks (SciERC, CoNLL-2003, WNUT-17) and open-weight models (Qwen3-8B, Qwen3-4B, LLaMA-3.1-8B).\nCoNLL-2003 is derived from the Reuters RCV1 corpus, which is available for research purposes under a NIST agreement; we use the standard research distribution.\nNo new human data was collected or annotated for this study.\nOur findings on signal limitations could inform more reliable deployment of IE systems by guiding practitioners toward appropriate confidence signals for their use case.\nCode and data will be released upon acceptance at [anonymized URL].\n\n\\section*{Responsible NLP Research Checklist}\n\\input{sections/checklist}\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{sections/appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

```text
1	% Degeneracy Upper Bound on Self-Consistency Gain
2	% Standalone theorem + proof for inclusion in paper
3	
4	\subsection{Information-Theoretic Upper Bound on SC Gain}
5	\label{sec:degeneracy-bound}
6	
7	We formalize the intuition that degeneracy---the phenomenon where all $N$ sampled outputs are identical---imposes a fundamental ceiling on the gain achievable by any self-consistency aggregation method.
8	
9	\begin{definition}[Instance-Level Degeneracy]
10	\label{def:degeneracy}
11	Given a generative model $\mathcal{M}$, an input instance $x$, and $N$ i.i.d.\ samples $\{y_1, \ldots, y_N\} \sim \mathcal{M}(\cdot \mid x)$, instance $x$ is \emph{degenerate} if $y_1 = y_2 = \cdots = y_N$. The \emph{degeneracy rate} of $\mathcal{M}$ on dataset $\mathcal{D}$ is
12	\[
13	D \;=\; \frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|} \sum_{x \in \mathcal{D}} \mathbb{1}\!\bigl[y_1(x) = \cdots = y_N(x)\bigr].
14	\]
15	\end{definition}
16	
17	\begin{definition}[Oracle Gap]
18	\label{def:oracle-gap}
19	The \emph{non-degenerate oracle gap} is the expected F1 improvement achievable by an oracle selector on non-degenerate instances:
20	\[
21	G_{\text{nd}} \;=\; \mathbb{E}\!\bigl[F_1^{\text{oracle}}(x) - F_1^{\text{greedy}}(x) \;\big|\; x \in \mathcal{D}_{\neg D}\bigr],
22	\]
23	where $\mathcal{D}_{\neg D} = \{x \in \mathcal{D} : x \text{ is non-degenerate}\}$ and $F_1^{\text{oracle}}(x) = \max_{i \in [N]} F_1(y_i(x), \hat{y}(x))$ selects the sample maximizing instance-level F1 against the gold label $\hat{y}(x)$.
24	\end{definition}
25	
26	\begin{theorem}[Degeneracy Upper Bound]
27	\label{thm:degeneracy-bound}
28	Let $A$ be any aggregation method that maps $N$ samples to a single output (including selection, construction, and voting methods). Then the expected F1 gain of $A$ over greedy decoding satisfies:
29	\begin{equation}
30	\label{eq:weak-bound}
31	\mathbb{E}\!\bigl[\Delta F_1(A)\bigr] \;\leq\; (1 - D) \cdot G_{\emph{nd}}.
32	\end{equation}
33	\end{theorem}
34	
35	\begin{proof}
36	Partition $\mathcal{D}$ into degenerate instances $\mathcal{D}_D$ (fraction $D$) and non-degenerate instances $\mathcal{D}_{\neg D}$ (fraction $1-D$). By the law of total expectation:
37	\begin{align}
38	\mathbb{E}[\Delta F_1(A)]
39	&= D \cdot \mathbb{E}[\Delta F_1(A) \mid x \in \mathcal{D}_D] \;+\; (1-D) \cdot \mathbb{E}[\Delta F_1(A) \mid x \in \mathcal{D}_{\neg D}]. \label{eq:decomp}
40	\end{align}
41	
42	\textbf{Term 1 (degenerate instances).}
43	For $x \in \mathcal{D}_D$, all $N$ samples are identical: $y_1 = \cdots = y_N = y^*$. Any deterministic aggregation $A$ can only output $y^*$ (or a subset/superset of its entities, in the case of construction methods). Since $y^*$ equals the greedy output, $A(x) = \text{greedy}(x)$, hence $\Delta F_1(A, x) = 0$ for all $x \in \mathcal{D}_D$.\footnote{For construction methods that threshold entity inclusion, the constructed output on degenerate instances equals the greedy output because every entity receives the maximum possible vote (all $N$ samples agree).}
44	
45	\textbf{Term 2 (non-degenerate instances).}
46	For $x \in \mathcal{D}_{\neg D}$, the gain of $A$ is bounded by the oracle gain:
47	\[
48	\Delta F_1(A, x) \;\leq\; F_1^{\text{oracle}}(x) - F_1^{\text{greedy}}(x).
49	\]
50	Taking expectations over non-degenerate instances:
51	\[
52	\mathbb{E}[\Delta F_1(A) \mid x \in \mathcal{D}_{\neg D}] \;\leq\; G_{\text{nd}}.
53	\]
54	
55	Substituting into~\eqref{eq:decomp}:
56	\[
57	\mathbb{E}[\Delta F_1(A)] \;\leq\; D \cdot 0 + (1-D) \cdot G_{\text{nd}} \;=\; (1-D) \cdot G_{\text{nd}}. \qedhere
58	\]
59	\end{proof}
60	
61	\begin{theorem}[Signal-Adjusted Bound]
62	\label{thm:signal-bound}
63	Let $s: \{y_1, \ldots, y_N\} \to \mathbb{R}^N$ be a quality signal used to weight or select among samples (e.g., log-probability, vote count, set Jaccard). Define the \emph{signal alignment} $\alpha \in [0, 1]$ as the fraction of the oracle gap recovered by signal-based aggregation on non-degenerate instances:
64	\[
65	\alpha \;=\; \frac{\mathbb{E}[\Delta F_1(A_s) \mid x \in \mathcal{D}_{\neg D}]}{G_{\emph{nd}}}.
66	\]
67	Then:
68	\begin{equation}
69	\label{eq:signal-bound}
70	\mathbb{E}[\Delta F_1(A_s)] \;=\; (1-D) \cdot G_{\emph{nd}} \cdot \alpha.
71	\end{equation}
72	\end{theorem}
73	
74	\begin{proof}
75	Direct substitution of the definition of $\alpha$ into the decomposition~\eqref{eq:decomp}:
76	\[
77	\mathbb{E}[\Delta F_1(A_s)] = (1-D) \cdot \mathbb{E}[\Delta F_1(A_s) \mid \mathcal{D}_{\neg D}] = (1-D) \cdot G_{\text{nd}} \cdot \alpha. \qedhere
78	\]
79	\end{proof}
80	
81	\begin{corollary}[Critical Degeneracy Threshold]
82	\label{cor:theta-critical}
83	For the expected SC gain to exceed a statistical significance threshold $\varepsilon > 0$, the degeneracy rate must satisfy:
84	\begin{equation}
85	\label{eq:theta-critical}
86	D \;<\; \theta_{\emph{crit}} \;\stackrel{\text{def}}{=}\; 1 - \frac{\varepsilon}{G_{\emph{nd}} \cdot \alpha}.
87	\end{equation}
88	When $D \geq \theta_{\emph{crit}}$, no signal-based SC method can yield statistically significant improvement.
89	\end{corollary}
90	
91	\begin{proof}
92	From Theorem~\ref{thm:signal-bound}, $\mathbb{E}[\Delta F_1] \geq \varepsilon$ requires $(1-D) \cdot G_{\text{nd}} \cdot \alpha \geq \varepsilon$, i.e., $D \leq 1 - \varepsilon / (G_{\text{nd}} \cdot \alpha)$.
93	\end{proof}
94	
95	\paragraph{Tighter bound via partial degeneracy.}
96	The binary partition in Theorem~\ref{thm:degeneracy-bound} is conservative: it treats all non-degenerate instances as equally exploitable. A tighter analysis considers \emph{partial degeneracy}---the fraction $k/N$ of samples identical to the mode.
97	
98	\begin{definition}[Effective Diversity]
99	\label{def:effective-diversity}
100	For instance $x$ with samples $\{y_1, \ldots, y_N\}$, let $m(x)$ be the mode output and $k(x) = |\{i : y_i = m(x)\}|$ the mode count. The \emph{effective diversity} is
101	\[
102	\eta(x) = 1 - \frac{k(x)}{N}.
103	\]
104	Note $\eta(x) = 0$ iff $x$ is degenerate, and $\eta(x) \leq 1 - 1/N$.
105	\end{definition}
106	
107	\begin{proposition}[Diversity-Weighted Bound]
108	\label{prop:diversity-bound}
109	Under the assumption that the oracle gap on instance $x$ scales with effective diversity:
110	\[
111	F_1^{\emph{oracle}}(x) - F_1^{\emph{greedy}}(x) \;\leq\; c \cdot \eta(x)
112	\]
113	for some constant $c > 0$, we obtain:
114	\begin{equation}
115	\label{eq:diversity-bound}
116	\mathbb{E}[\Delta F_1(A)] \;\leq\; c \cdot \mathbb{E}[\eta(x)] \cdot \alpha.
117	\end{equation}
118	Since $\mathbb{E}[\eta(x)] \leq (1-D)(1-1/N)$, this refines the bound in~\eqref{eq:signal-bound} when partial degeneracy is prevalent.
119	\end{proposition}
120	
121	\paragraph{Finite-sample correction.}
122	With $N$ i.i.d.\ samples from a model with per-instance generation entropy $H(x)$, the probability that all $N$ samples are identical is $p_{\text{degen}}(x) = \sum_y p(y \mid x)^N$. For high-confidence instances (one output dominates), $p_{\text{degen}} \approx p_{\max}^N \to 1$ exponentially in $N$. Thus:
123	\begin{equation}
124	\label{eq:finite-sample}
125	D(N) = \mathbb{E}_x\!\left[\sum_y p(y \mid x)^N\right] \;\xrightarrow{N \to \infty}\; \Pr[H(x) = 0],
126	\end{equation}
127	which shows that increasing $N$ cannot reduce degeneracy below the fraction of deterministic instances. In the limit, SC gain is bounded by the fraction of instances with non-zero generation entropy.
128	
129	\paragraph{Numerical instantiation.}
130	For $N = 8$ with empirically observed parameters from our experiments:
131	\begin{itemize}
132	\item \textbf{Low degeneracy} (SciERC, FewNERD FT): $D \approx 0.12$--$0.21$, $G_{\text{nd}} \approx 14$--$15$ pp. Bound: $(1-D) \cdot G_{\text{nd}} \approx 11$--$13$ pp. With empirical $\alpha \approx 0.08$--$0.11$, predicted gain $\approx 0.9$--$1.5$ pp, matching observed $\Delta \approx 0.4$--$1.6$ pp.
133	
134	\item \textbf{High degeneracy} (CoNLL FT): $D \approx 0.63$, $G_{\text{nd}} \approx 10$--$11$ pp. Bound: $(1-D) \cdot G_{\text{nd}} \approx 3.7$--$4.0$ pp. With $\alpha \approx 0.08$, predicted gain $\approx 0.3$ pp $< \varepsilon$, consistent with observed negative $\Delta$.
135	
136	\item \textbf{Critical threshold}: With $\varepsilon = 0.3$ pp (our significance cutoff), $G_{\text{nd}} = 12$ pp, and $\alpha = 0.10$:
137	$\theta_{\text{crit}} = 1 - 0.3 / (12.0 \times 0.10) = 0.75$. Configurations with $D > 0.75$ are predicted to show no significant SC gain, consistent with CoNLL-FT and LLaMA-CoNLL configurations ($D = 0.60$--$0.90$) where all methods underperform greedy.
138	\end{itemize}
139	
```

Any deterministic aggregation $A$ can only output $y^*$ (or a subset/superset of its entities, in the case of construction methods). Since $y^*$ equals the greedy output, $A(x) = \\text{greedy}(x)$, hence $\\Delta F_1(A, x) = 0$ for all $x \\in \\mathcal{D}_D$.\\footnote{For construction methods that threshold entity inclusion, the constructed output on degenerate instances equals the greedy output because every entity receives the maximum possible vote (all $N$ samples agree).}\n\n\\textbf{Term 2 (non-degenerate instances).}\nFor $x \\in \\mathcal{D}_{\\neg D}$, the gain of $A$ is bounded by the oracle gain:\n\\[\n\\Delta F_1(A, x) \\;\\leq\\; F_1^{\\text{oracle}}(x) - F_1^{\\text{greedy}}(x).\n\\]\nTaking expectations over non-degenerate instances:\n\\[\n\\mathbb{E}[\\Delta F_1(A) \\mid x \\in \\mathcal{D}_{\\neg D}] \\;\\leq\\; G_{\\text{nd}}.\n\\]\n\nSubstituting into~\\eqref{eq:decomp}:\n\\[\n\\mathbb{E}[\\Delta F_1(A)] \\;\\leq\\; D \\cdot 0 + (1-D) \\cdot G_{\\text{nd}} \\;=\\; (1-D) \\cdot G_{\\text{nd}}. \\qedhere\n\\]\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\begin{theorem}[Signal-Adjusted Bound]\n\\label{thm:signal-bound}\nLet $s: \\{y_1, \\ldots, y_N\\} \\to \\mathbb{R}^N$ be a quality signal used to weight or select among samples (e.g., log-probability, vote count, set Jaccard). Define the \\emph{signal alignment} $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$ as the fraction of the oracle gap recovered by signal-based aggregation on non-degenerate instances:\n\\[\n\\alpha \\;=\\; \\frac{\\mathbb{E}[\\Delta F_1(A_s) \\mid x \\in \\mathcal{D}_{\\neg D}]}{G_{\\emph{nd}}}.\n\\]\nThen:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:signal-bound}\n\\mathbb{E}[\\Delta F_1(A_s)] \\;=\\; (1-D) \\cdot G_{\\emph{nd}} \\cdot \\alpha.\n\\end{equation}\n\\end{theorem}\n\n\\begin{proof}\nDirect substitution of the definition of $\\alpha$ into the decomposition~\\eqref{eq:decomp}:\n\\[\n\\mathbb{E}[\\Delta F_1(A_s)] = (1-D) \\cdot \\mathbb{E}[\\Delta F_1(A_s) \\mid \\mathcal{D}_{\\neg D}] = (1-D) \\cdot G_{\\text{nd}} \\cdot \\alpha. \\qedhere\n\\]\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\begin{corollary}[Critical Degeneracy Threshold]\n\\label{cor:theta-critical}\nFor the expected SC gain to exceed a statistical significance threshold $\\varepsilon > 0$, the degeneracy rate must satisfy:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:theta-critical}\nD \\;<\\; \\theta_{\\emph{crit}} \\;\\stackrel{\\text{def}}{=}\\; 1 - \\frac{\\varepsilon}{G_{\\emph{nd}} \\cdot \\alpha}.\n\\end{equation}\nWhen $D \\geq \\theta_{\\emph{crit}}$, no signal-based SC method can yield statistically significant improvement.\n\\end{corollary}\n\n\\begin{proof}\nFrom Theorem~\\ref{thm:signal-bound}, $\\mathbb{E}[\\Delta F_1] \\geq \\varepsilon$ requires $(1-D) \\cdot G_{\\text{nd}} \\cdot \\alpha \\geq \\varepsilon$, i.e., $D \\leq 1 - \\varepsilon / (G_{\\text{nd}} \\cdot \\alpha)$.\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\paragraph{Tighter bound via partial degeneracy.}\nThe binary partition in Theorem~\\ref{thm:degeneracy-bound} is conservative: it treats all non-degenerate instances as equally exploitable. A tighter analysis considers \\emph{partial degeneracy}---the fraction $k/N$ of samples identical to the mode.\n\n\\begin{definition}[Effective Diversity]\n\\label{def:effective-diversity}\nFor instance $x$ with samples $\\{y_1, \\ldots, y_N\\}$, let $m(x)$ be the mode output and $k(x) = |\\{i : y_i = m(x)\\}|$ the mode count. The \\emph{effective diversity} is\n\\[\n\\eta(x) = 1 - \\frac{k(x)}{N}.\n\\]\nNote $\\eta(x) = 0$ iff $x$ is degenerate, and $\\eta(x) \\leq 1 - 1/N$.\n\\end{definition}\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Diversity-Weighted Bound]\n\\label{prop:diversity-bound}\nUnder the assumption that the oracle gap on instance $x$ scales with effective diversity:\n\\[\nF_1^{\\emph{oracle}}(x) - F_1^{\\emph{greedy}}(x) \\;\\leq\\; c \\cdot \\eta(x)\n\\]\nfor some constant $c > 0$, we obtain:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:diversity-bound}\n\\mathbb{E}[\\Delta F_1(A)] \\;\\leq\\; c \\cdot \\mathbb{E}[\\eta(x)] \\cdot \\alpha.\n\\end{equation}\nSince $\\mathbb{E}[\\eta(x)] \\leq (1-D)(1-1/N)$, this refines the bound in~\\eqref{eq:signal-bound} when partial degeneracy is prevalent.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\paragraph{Finite-sample correction.}\nWith $N$ i.i.d.\\ samples from a model with per-instance generation entropy $H(x)$, the probability that all $N$ samples are identical is $p_{\\text{degen}}(x) = \\sum_y p(y \\mid x)^N$. For high-confidence instances (one output dominates), $p_{\\text{degen}} \\approx p_{\\max}^N \\to 1$ exponentially in $N$. Thus:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:finite-sample}\nD(N) = \\mathbb{E}_x\\!\\left[\\sum_y p(y \\mid x)^N\\right] \\;\\xrightarrow{N \\to \\infty}\\; \\Pr[H(x) = 0],\n\\end{equation}\nwhich shows that increasing $N$ cannot reduce degeneracy below the fraction of deterministic instances. In the limit, SC gain is bounded by the fraction of instances with non-zero generation entropy.\n\n\\paragraph{Numerical instantiation.}\nFor $N = 8$ with empirically observed parameters from our experiments:\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item \\textbf{Low degeneracy} (SciERC, FewNERD FT): $D \\approx 0.12$--$0.21$, $G_{\\text{nd}} \\approx 14$--$15$ pp. Bound: $(1-D) \\cdot G_{\\text{nd}} \\approx 11$--$13$ pp. With empirical $\\alpha \\approx 0.08$--$0.11$, predicted gain $\\approx 0.9$--$1.5$ pp, matching observed $\\Delta \\approx 0.4$--$1.6$ pp.\n\n\\item \\textbf{High degeneracy} (CoNLL FT): $D \\approx 0.63$, $G_{\\text{nd}} \\approx 10$--$11$ pp. Bound: $(1-D) \\cdot G_{\\text{nd}} \\approx 3.7$--$4.0$ pp. With $\\alpha \\approx 0.08$, predicted gain $\\approx 0.3$ pp $< \\varepsilon$, consistent with observed negative $\\Delta$.\n\n\\item \\textbf{Critical threshold}: With $\\varepsilon = 0.3$ pp (our significance cutoff), $G_{\\text{nd}} = 12$ pp, and $\\alpha = 0.10$:\n$\\theta_{\\text{crit}} = 1 - 0.3 / (12.0 \\times 0.10) = 0.75$. Configurations with $D > 0.75$ are predicted to show no significant SC gain, consistent with CoNLL-FT and LLaMA-CoNLL configurations ($D = 0.60$--$0.90$) where all methods underperform greedy.\n\\end{itemize}\n",

    }
